People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Yeah, about a year and two months.\n\nDoctor: Before this year and two months ago, have you ever experienced a period like this where you felt depressed or lost interest in things for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I've had bad days before, like anyone, but... never anything like this. Not where it just stretched on and on. Before the divorce and the move, I was always pretty active and involved. So, no, I haven't had a time like this before.\n\nDoctor: When you moved into the smaller rental after your divorce, did your mood and loss of interest in activities start right away, or did it take some time before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess it didn't hit me all at once. And then I stopped going to the coaching sessions. So, it kind of crept up on me over a few weeks after the move, rather than happening the very first day.\n\nDoctor: During these past 14 months, have there been any periods—even a week or two—where your mood lifted, you felt more like yourself, or you had more energy and motivation to do things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, there hasn't been any time where I felt like things were back to normal. It's just been a steady... low, I guess. I haven't had any days or weeks where I suddenly felt energized or wanted to get back out there. It's just been pretty much the same this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, do you think your depression was directly caused by the divorce and moving, or do you feel like it might have happened anyway even if those life events hadn't occurred?\n\nPatient: I don't know for sure. I mean, it's hard to separate it all out. The divorce and the move... they were huge. Everything changed. So it feels like that's what caused it. But... I don't know if I would have felt this way anyway. It's just... it all happened at once, and I've been stuck here ever since. I really couldn't say if it would have happened without all that.\n\nDoctor: If the divorce and move hadn't happened, do you think you would still have developed this depression, or do you believe you'd be doing fine right now?\n\nPatient: I mean... I'd like to think I'd be doing fine right now. If things had just stayed the way they were, if I was still in my old house... yeah, maybe I wouldn't be in this hole. But to be honest with you, I really can't give you a definite answer. Like I said, it's just impossible for me to guess how things would have played out if none of that happened.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the depression started creeping up a few weeks after the move. So if I'm understanding the timeline correctly, you moved about 14 months ago, and the low mood and loss of interest began roughly a month after that—so around 13 months ago. Is that accurate?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that sounds about right. The move was... let's see, yeah, 14 months ago. And it was maybe three or four weeks after I got settled in the new place that I just started feeling... flat. So, 13 months of this. Yeah, that timeline is accurate.\n\nDoctor: Looking back on everything that happened—the divorce, the move, all of it—do you feel like your depression is about what you'd expect given how difficult those events were, or does it feel like the depression is worse or more intense than what those events alone should have caused?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure how to gauge that, to be honest. I know there are things I need to get done, but I just can't seem to make myself start.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice that you stopped enjoying these activities and started feeling like you weren't yourself?\n\nPatient: I suppose things have just been unraveling since I moved into this smaller rental. That was right after my divorce... so, I guess it's been about a year and two months ago now. Yeah, about a year and two months.\n\nDoctor: Before this year and two months ago, have you ever experienced a period like this where you felt depressed or lost interest in things for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I've had bad days before, like anyone, but... never anything like this. Not where it just stretched on and on. Before the divorce and the move, I was always pretty active and involved. So, no, I haven't had a time like this before.\n\nDoctor: When you moved into the smaller rental after your divorce, did your mood and loss of interest in activities start right away, or did it take some time before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess it didn't hit me all at once. When I first moved in, I was just trying to get settled and deal with all the paperwork from the divorce. But... maybe after a month or so, I realized I just didn't want to unpack my guitar. And then I stopped going to the coaching sessions. So, it kind of crept up on me over a few weeks after the move, rather than happening the very first day.\n\nDoctor: During these past 14 months, have there been any periods—even a week or two—where your mood lifted, you felt more like yourself, or you had more energy and motivation to do things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, there hasn't been any time where I felt like things were back to normal. It's just been a steady... low, I guess. I haven't had any days or weeks where I suddenly felt energized or wanted to get back out there. It's just been pretty much the same this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, do you think your depression was directly caused by the divorce and moving, or do you feel like it might have happened anyway even if those life events hadn't occurred?\n\nPatient: I don't know for sure. I mean, it's hard to separate it all out. The divorce and the move... they were huge. Everything changed. So it feels like that's what caused it. But... I don't know if I would have felt this way anyway. It's just... it all happened at once, and I've been stuck here ever since. I really couldn't say if it would have happened without all that.\n\nDoctor: If the divorce and move hadn't happened, do you think you would still have developed this depression, or do you believe you'd be doing fine right now?\n\nPatient: I mean... I'd like to think I'd be doing fine right now. If things had just stayed the way they were, if I was still in my old house... yeah, maybe I wouldn't be in this hole. But to be honest with you, I really can't give you a definite answer. Like I said, it's just impossible for me to guess how things would have played out if none of that happened.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the depression started creeping up a few weeks after the move. So if I'm understanding the timeline correctly, you moved about 14 months ago, and the low mood and loss of interest began roughly a month after that—so around 13 months ago. Is that accurate?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that sounds about right. The move was... let's see, yeah, 14 months ago. And it was maybe three or four weeks after I got settled in the new place that I just started feeling... flat. So, 13 months of this. Yeah, that timeline is accurate.\n\nDoctor: Looking back on everything that happened—the divorce, the move, all of it—do you feel like your depression is about what you'd expect given how difficult those events were, or does it feel like the depression is worse or more intense than what those events alone should have caused?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure how to gauge that, to be honest. I mean... going through a divorce and having to uproot your whole life, you expect it to knock you down. It's a heavy hit. \n\nBut as for whether this is worse or more intense than it should be... I don't know. I've never been through something like this before, so I don't really have a baseline for what's expected. I just know it feels bad, and I haven't bounced back. I couldn't tell you if it's out of proportion or not.\n\nDoctor: If your living situation improved significantly—say, if you were able to move back to your old house or find a new place you really loved—do you think your mood and motivation would bounce back to how you were before all this happened, or do you worry that you'd still feel this way regardless?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I really don't. It's hard to say if moving back would fix it. Sometimes I think if I was just back in my old house, things would be normal again. But then... I don't know if a house can fix how empty I feel right now. I worry that even if I moved, this... this heaviness would just come with me. I'm just not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

I know there are things I need to get done, but I just can't seem to make myself start.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice that you stopped enjoying these activities and started feeling like you weren't yourself?\n\nPatient: I suppose things have just been unraveling since I moved into this smaller rental. That was right after my divorce... so, I guess it's been about a year and two months ago now. Yeah, about a year and two months.\n\nDoctor: Before this year and two months ago, have you ever experienced a period like this where you felt depressed or lost interest in things for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I've had bad days before, like anyone, but... never anything like this. Not where it just stretched on and on. Before the divorce and the move, I was always pretty active and involved. So, no, I haven't had a time like this before.\n\nDoctor: When you moved into the smaller rental after your divorce, did your mood and loss of interest in activities start right away, or did it take some time before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess it didn't hit me all at once. When I first moved in, I was just trying to get settled and deal with all the paperwork from the divorce. But... maybe after a month or so, I realized I just didn't want to unpack my guitar. And then I stopped going to the coaching sessions. So, it kind of crept up on me over a few weeks after the move, rather than happening the very first day.\n\nDoctor: During these past 14 months, have there been any periods—even a week or two—where your mood lifted, you felt more like yourself, or you had more energy and motivation to do things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, there hasn't been any time where I felt like things were back to normal. It's just been a steady... low, I guess. I haven't had any days or weeks where I suddenly felt energized or wanted to get back out there. It's just been pretty much the same this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, do you think your depression was directly caused by the divorce and moving, or do you feel like it might have happened anyway even if those life events hadn't occurred?\n\nPatient: I don't know for sure. I mean, it's hard to separate it all out. The divorce and the move... they were huge. Everything changed. So it feels like that's what caused it. But... I don't know if I would have felt this way anyway. It's just... it all happened at once, and I've been stuck here ever since. I really couldn't say if it would have happened without all that.\n\nDoctor: If the divorce and move hadn't happened, do you think you would still have developed this depression, or do you believe you'd be doing fine right now?\n\nPatient: I mean... I'd like to think I'd be doing fine right now. If things had just stayed the way they were, if I was still in my old house... yeah, maybe I wouldn't be in this hole. But to be honest with you, I really can't give you a definite answer. Like I said, it's just impossible for me to guess how things would have played out if none of that happened.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the depression started creeping up a few weeks after the move. So if I'm understanding the timeline correctly, you moved about 14 months ago, and the low mood and loss of interest began roughly a month after that—so around 13 months ago. Is that accurate?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that sounds about right. The move was... let's see, yeah, 14 months ago. And it was maybe three or four weeks after I got settled in the new place that I just started feeling... flat. So, 13 months of this. Yeah, that timeline is accurate.\n\nDoctor: Looking back on everything that happened—the divorce, the move, all of it—do you feel like your depression is about what you'd expect given how difficult those events were, or does it feel like the depression is worse or more intense than what those events alone should have caused?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure how to gauge that, to be honest. I mean... going through a divorce and having to uproot your whole life, you expect it to knock you down. It's a heavy hit. \n\nBut as for whether this is worse or more intense than it should be... I don't know. I've never been through something like this before, so I don't really have a baseline for what's expected. I just know it feels bad, and I haven't bounced back. I couldn't tell you if it's out of proportion or not.\n\nDoctor: If your living situation improved significantly—say, if you were able to move back to your old house or find a new place you really loved—do you think your mood and motivation would bounce back to how you were before all this happened, or do you worry that you'd still feel this way regardless?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I really don't. It's hard to say if moving back would fix it. Sometimes I think if I was just back in my old house, things would be normal again. But then... I don't know if a house can fix how empty I feel right now. I worry that even if I moved, this... this heaviness would just come with me. I'm just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Since you moved 14 months ago, have you noticed any changes in your sleep, appetite, or energy level—things like sleeping much more or less than usual, significant weight changes, or feeling physically exhausted even when you haven't done much?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any big changes with my sleep or appetite. I mean, I guess I'm tired, but... I haven't been sleeping all day or staying up all night or anything like that. And my weight is about the same. I just... I don't really have the energy to do much, but I wouldn't say I'm physically exhausted, just... unmotivated, I guess. So, no, nothing major like what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the divorce and the move now, do those events still feel like the main reason you're depressed, or has the depression started to feel like its own separate thing that exists apart from what happened?\n\nPatient: I don't really know how to separate it, to be honest. I mean, I haven't thought about it like that before. \n\nThe divorce and having to move... they happened, and I feel the way I do now. I just know I still feel it.\n\nDoctor: If we could somehow undo the divorce and move—if you could go back to exactly how things were before all that happened—do you think your depression would go away, or do you suspect it would stick around even if those events hadn't occurred?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. That's a hard question to answer because I can't really imagine it. I guess... I'd like to think if none of that happened, I wouldn't feel this way. But honestly, I'm not sure. So, it kind of crept up on me over a few weeks after the move, rather than happening the very first day.\n\nDoctor: During these past 14 months, have there been any periods—even a week or two—where your mood lifted, you felt more like yourself, or you had more energy and motivation to do things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, there hasn't been any time where I felt like things were back to normal. It's just been a steady... low, I guess. I haven't had any days or weeks where I suddenly felt energized or wanted to get back out there. It's just been pretty much the same this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, do you think your depression was directly caused by the divorce and moving, or do you feel like it might have happened anyway even if those life events hadn't occurred?\n\nPatient: I don't know for sure. I mean, it's hard to separate it all out. The divorce and the move... they were huge. Everything changed. So it feels like that's what caused it. But... I don't know if I would have felt this way anyway. It's just... it all happened at once, and I've been stuck here ever since. I really couldn't say if it would have happened without all that.\n\nDoctor: If the divorce and move hadn't happened, do you think you would still have developed this depression, or do you believe you'd be doing fine right now?\n\nPatient: I mean... I'd like to think I'd be doing fine right now. If things had just stayed the way they were, if I was still in my old house... yeah, maybe I wouldn't be in this hole. So if I'm understanding the timeline correctly, you moved about 14 months ago, and the low mood and loss of interest began roughly a month after that—so around 13 months ago. And it was maybe three or four weeks after I got settled in the new place that I just started feeling... flat. So, 13 months of this. Yeah, that timeline is accurate.\n\nDoctor: Looking back on everything that happened—the divorce, the move, all of it—do you feel like your depression is about what you'd expect given how difficult those events were, or does it feel like the depression is worse or more intense than what those events alone should have caused?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure how to gauge that, to be honest. I mean... going through a divorce and having to uproot your whole life, you expect it to knock you down. It's a heavy hit. \n\nBut as for whether this is worse or more intense than it should be... I don't know. I've never been through something like this before, so I don't really have a baseline for what's expected. I just know it feels bad, and I haven't bounced back. I couldn't tell you if it's out of proportion or not.\n\nDoctor: If your living situation improved significantly—say, if you were able to move back to your old house or find a new place you really loved—do you think your mood and motivation would bounce back to how you were before all this happened, or do you worry that you'd still feel this way regardless?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I really don't. It's hard to say if moving back would fix it. Sometimes I think if I was just back in my old house, things would be normal again. But then... I don't know if a house can fix how empty I feel right now. I worry that even if I moved, this... this heaviness would just come with me. I'm just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Since you moved 14 months ago, have you noticed any changes in your sleep, appetite, or energy level—things like sleeping much more or less than usual, significant weight changes, or feeling physically exhausted even when you haven't done much?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any big changes with my sleep or appetite. I mean, I guess I'm tired, but... I haven't been sleeping all day or staying up all night or anything like that. And my weight is about the same. I just... I don't really have the energy to do much, but I wouldn't say I'm physically exhausted, just... unmotivated, I guess. I just know I still feel it.\n\nDoctor: If we could somehow undo the divorce and move—if you could go back to exactly how things were before all that happened—do you think your depression would go away, or do you suspect it would stick around even if those events hadn't occurred?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. That's a hard question to answer because I can't really imagine it. I guess... I'd like to think if none of that happened, I wouldn't feel this way. But honestly, I'm not sure. It's been going on for a while now, and I just don't know if going back in time would just erase it all. I couldn't tell you if it's out of proportion or not.\n\nDoctor: If your living situation improved significantly—say, if you were able to move back to your old house or find a new place you really loved—do you think your mood and motivation would bounce back to how you were before all this happened, or do you worry that you'd still feel this way regardless?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I really don't. It's hard to say if moving back would fix it. Sometimes I think if I was just back in my old house, things would be normal again. But then... I don't know if a house can fix how empty I feel right now. I worry that even if I moved, this... this heaviness would just come with me. I'm just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Since you moved 14 months ago, have you noticed any changes in your sleep, appetite, or energy level—things like sleeping much more or less than usual, significant weight changes, or feeling physically exhausted even when you haven't done much?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any big changes with my sleep or appetite. I mean, I guess I'm tired, but... I haven't been sleeping all day or staying up all night or anything like that. And my weight is about the same. I just... I don't really have the energy to do much, but I wouldn't say I'm physically exhausted, just... unmotivated, I guess. So, no, nothing major like what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the divorce and the move now, do those events still feel like the main reason you're depressed, or has the depression started to feel like its own separate thing that exists apart from what happened?\n\nPatient: I don't really know how to separate it, to be honest. I just know I still feel it.\n\nDoctor: If we could somehow undo the divorce and move—if you could go back to exactly how things were before all that happened—do you think your depression would go away, or do you suspect it would stick around even if those events hadn't occurred?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. That's a hard question to answer because I can't really imagine it. I guess... I'd like to think if none of that happened, I wouldn't feel this way. But honestly, I'm not sure. It's been going on for a while now, and I just don't know if going back in time would just erase it all. I really couldn't say for certain.\n\nDoctor: Let me ask this differently: Right now, if you were offered a real opportunity—like a great job in your old town, or a chance to reconnect with friends and activities you loved—would you feel motivated to pursue it, or do you think this heaviness and lack of motivation would prevent you from going after it even if the opportunity was genuinely good?\n\nPatient: I think... the heaviness would probably prevent me. Mostly I just don't feel like myself anymore. I've stopped doing all the things I used to care about. I stopped hiking, coaching, playing guitar... I don't even call people back. So even if a really great opportunity came along, I don't think I could go after it. Some days I just sit in that small rental for hours. I know there are things I need to do, but... I just can't make myself start.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at your life before the divorce and move, did you ever struggle with depression or low mood even during good times, or is this the very first time in your life you've experienced anything like this?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. So, yeah, I'd say this is the first time it's ever been like this.\n\nDoctor: Thinking about other people you know who've gone through divorces and major life changes—do you think most of them would still be feeling this depressed and unmotivated after 13 months, or do you think most people would have started to recover by now?\n\nPatient: I... I honestly don't know. I guess I haven't really thought about how other people handle it. I mean, I know a year is a long time, so maybe most people would be moving on by now. But I just don't know what's normal for this kind of thing. I just know I haven't been able to shake it.\n\nDoctor: If we could somehow completely resolve the divorce situation and you moved into a place you absolutely loved tomorrow, do you think your depression would go away relatively quickly—like within a few weeks or months—or do you worry that even with those problems solved, this low mood and lack of motivation would persist?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure. I mean, it sounds nice to think that fixing those things would make it all go away. But right now, this feeling is just so deep. It's just... here.\n\nDoctor: When you imagine yourself in a completely different life scenario—say, you never got married, never went through a divorce, and never had to move—do you think you'd still be struggling with this same kind of depression and emptiness, or do you think this feeling is really only happening because of everything that fell apart?\n\nPatient: I...
